The final **sidecar-only** transaction atomically installs the complete captured generation, full validation record and projection pointer under the maintenance call's independently authorized service grant, after the final pre-commit canonical/dependency fence check. A kill before this commit leaves the previous pointer or an incomplete checkpoint; it cannot install a publication without its matching certificate. The certificate attests the verified captured generation, **not present request readiness** (§6.1.2). Ordinary canonical commits may race the sidecar COMMIT; there is no authority lease or cross-database atomicity. Exactly one coordinated maintenance writer owns a projection; every structural/data update invalidates affected validation evidence or replaces it atomically. An independently certified vector manifest binds the same lexical publication and exact embedder identity; vector refresh cannot invalidate or rewrite unchanged lexical statistics under a certificate.

After per-call authorization, a reader performs only: safe descriptor/identity and scalar physical-format/schema checks; one keyed projection-pointer lookup; one keyed validation-record lookup; bounded decoding/HMAC/binding comparison; scalar canonical/access/dependency fence probes over ≤32 namespaces and existing declared-dependency bounds; and scalar comparison of pointer/generation/publication/checkpoint/incarnation. Read pointer/record/generation in the same sidecar read transaction and recheck publication/fences before final delivery. Counts come from the authenticated record, not request-time COUNT/postings/statistics scans. Reuse a valid record across fresh handles; no process-local warm certificate is required. Treat missing/mismatched record as honest unavailable.

Readiness invalidators are explicit: canonical file/store/schema identity or required restore rebuild; sidecar file identity/incarnation/schema/format; selected projection/types/intent; stable accessDigest including optional ownerGeneration; tokenizer/representation; canonical/dependency fence; publication/checkpoint/generation pointer; invalid record MAC/manifest binding; or an observed native corruption signal. A current-fence mismatch makes an otherwise intact certificate **stale for requests**, not a false attestation of its captured generation. Missing/invalid structural or cryptographic bindings remain unavailable, with the exact §6.1 reasons. Changes unrelated to the projection do not invalidate readiness. Same-database DML is trusted only through the coordinated maintenance writer; arbitrary same-user edits/undetected bit rot are not certified by a one-row read. SQLite read errors refuse and schedule **no** repair. This trust boundary cannot bypass canonical final refetch or either request/service currentness check. Recommended restore rebuilds sidecars, so copied certificates never become ready merely because they were backed up.

#### 6.1.2 Captured certification versus current readiness (revision-2 finding R2-1)

Let **E** be the exact captured canonical/dependency fence vector and verified eligible projection used by a certificate. Certification means its immutable documents/postings/statistics (and separately certified exact-identity vectors) agree with that captured generation. Readiness means those bindings **also match a fresh current canonical snapshot and stable accessDigest at final validation**, under the request or service grant that passed its two currentness checks. There is no cross-database atomic commit and no persistent “currently ready” bit. Neither a valid certificate nor a successful sidecar COMMIT alone establishes readiness. This content-readiness check adds no synchronized authority-revocation guarantee beyond §4.1.

Before installation, maintenance must actually verify the complete staging manifest against E and make its prescribed last fence check; it must never relabel E's data or vectors as E+1. If that check already observes a changed vector, retain/discard the staged work under the existing resume rules rather than knowingly installing it as current. However, the following race is explicitly permitted: check E; an ordinary WAL writer commits E+1; the sidecar commits E's certificate and pointer. The resulting publication is **certified for E and immediately stale**. Holding a canonical read snapshot cannot prevent that ordinary WAL commit. Such a publication is not a corrupt certificate, but **no stale generation is accepted as ready**.

Refresh completion is defined by this post-installation protocol, within the same hard operation deadline:

1. Under the maintenance call's independent service authority, complete all provider awaits, preparatory callbacks, accounting and diagnostic sampling, and confirm the sidecar commit outcome. Then run the **second synchronous service isCurrent check immediately before** the fresh final canonical transaction; changed/unverifiable service state returns `scope_changed`. There is no lease across installation/final validation, and no further host callback after that final sample/read. A committed artifact may survive a later authority failure/timeout/error; do not claim that public failure rolled it back or granted reader authority.
2. End every older canonical read transaction used for the pre-installation check, then start a **new deferred canonical transaction**. Its first actual canonical read acquires a fresh post-sidecar-commit snapshot; do not reuse the snapshot that observed E before installation. Under this snapshot compare all required canonical/dependency fences and physical/store bindings with the certificate, and verify the matching sidecar publication pointer/record under a sidecar read transaction. Use the certified counts, not deep scans. No host callback/provider/await occurs in these final transactions or after final reading.
3. **Refresh success linearizes at acquisition of that post-commit canonical snapshot**, conditional on the entire bounded comparison and both read transactions completing successfully before the public cutoff. For lexical mode, `status:'complete'` requires a matching complete lexical publication at that point. For embedding mode it additionally requires complete matching vector coverage for the selected identity at that same point; lexical-ready/vector-incomplete remains `partial` under the existing channel rules.
4. If the fresh snapshot sees E+1 while the installed certificate is for E, refresh returns successful **`status:'rebuilding'`**, with existing `coverage.status:'unavailable'`, `freshness:'stale'`, unknown current eligible/indexed counts null, and its actual durable publication progress. `getContextIndexStatus` reports stale (or a valid newer catching_up checkpoint); candidate/assembly reads return `index_unavailable`. No request searches the stale generation or repairs it. A native failure/abort/timeout instead keeps its own error; no incomplete comparison yields complete. A later explicit maintenance quantum may reconcile E+1.
5. If the post-commit snapshot still sees E, complete is truthful at that linearization point; a writer committing afterward can make subsequent operations stale without retroactively invalidating this completion. Every later status/read independently performs current-fence checks; it must not trust refresh's earlier complete response. Readers apply their existing final canonical/authority revalidation, not a maintenance-created readiness flag.

A no-op refresh that needs no new publication skips sidecar writes but applies the same final fresh-snapshot comparison to the existing certified pointer after its last maintenance work. It does not manufacture a new certificate or report complete from cached readiness. A status call likewise reports readiness only against its current canonical snapshot; the statement that refresh completed earlier is not validation evidence for that status call.

These rules apply even if a newly installed stale pointer displaced an older publication. They permit captured-generation certification, not stale service or altered authority guarantees. The deterministic rendezvous injects the ordinary writer **after maintenance's final pre-commit fence check and before sidecar COMMIT**, verifies the intact E certificate survives, then verifies post-commit refresh/status/read report E+1 staleness until explicit catch-up.

### 6.2 Explicit background API and budgets

```js
const maintainer = await openContextIndexMaintainer({
  supervisor,
  path, version: 'context-read-v1', profile: 'context-read-product-v1.1',
  readSet, ownerCoverage, host: { moduleUrl, configuration },
  sidecarJournalMode: 'WAL',        // required: 'DELETE' or 'WAL'
  deadlineMs: 5000
});

await maintainer.refreshContextIndex({
  readSet, types, intent,
  mode: 'lexical',                  // required: 'lexical' or 'embeddings'
  budget: { maxItems: 1024, maxMs: 5000 }
}, serviceCredential, { signal });
await maintainer.getContextIndexStatus({ readSet, types, intent, deadlineMs }, serviceCredential, { signal });
await maintainer.close();
```

The maintainer opens the **canonical database read-only** and only the product sidecar writable. It never constructs ordinary `openMemoryCore`, migrates canonical v18, changes canonical journal mode or installs hooks. Its separate service authority explicitly allowlists `refreshContextIndex` and refuses HTTP request credentials (§4.1.3). Read handles do not expose refresh; maintenance is neither an MCP tool nor a lookup fallback.

One refresh is one bounded quantum: `maxItems` 1–1,024 transformation/embedding attempts, `maxMs` 1–5,000 including opening/validation needed by that call, native waits, scans, checkpoint/publication and inference. Parent cutoff/cancellation follows §4.3. Existing batches cap at 128 rows/2,000,000 text units, with provider-aware single-document local batches where §20 requires them. Scanned metadata, support/dependency lookups and text bytes are independently metered; item caps alone do not bound scanning cost. No unbounded “drain until complete” call is exposed.

`mode:'lexical'` builds missing storage or catches up changed metadata/text and never calls an embedder, even when one is configured. `mode:'embeddings'` requires an independently explicit provider and maintenance policy/budget; it operates only on an already ready lexical projection, otherwise returns `rebuilding`/unavailable without implicitly performing lexical work. It embeds missing exact-identity document vectors outside transactions and revalidates immutable service-grant binding, deadline and canonical/index fences after every await and before each commit; service currentness has the entry/final samples in §4.1.3. Missing vector state never triggers implicit embeddings in a read.

Reuse checkpoint/replay and metadata-merge machinery. Put deep integrity/statistics verification, first schema creation, repair, full build, tokenization and catch-up in maintenance Workers. Read requests perform only bounded read-side identity/format/publication/freshness verification and ready-index lookup; never DDL, `quick_check`, full statistics consistency scans, tokenization of canonical document corpora, writes or repairs. Use §6.1.1's durable validation handoff: construction without a projection token does not read private counts, and an authorized fresh reader can validate the published record without maintenance. A new uncertified generation remains unavailable; an already certified one does not become unvalidated merely because the HTTP reader is fresh.

Checkpoints atomically commit sidecar documents/postings/statistics/vector changes and matching progress. Certify only a complete verified captured projection under independently checked service authority; ordinary canonical/dependency changes invalidate **readiness** at canonical commit, including feature-off writers and other processes. Service revocation has the bounded entry/final-check semantics of §4.1.3, not synchronized exclusion during publication. An immediately stale certified publication is allowed under §6.1.2; **no stale generation is accepted as ready**, and no partially updated statistics score. Killing an uncommitted batch leaves replayable work; committed checkpoints survive. Reuse valid unchanged text/vector identities, never relabel vectors across canonical revisions, captured generations or embedder/representation identities.

The product explicitly schedules maintenance from its per-minute maintenance cron (a product-internal endpoint). Prebuild **`types:['memory','card']` × `intent:'current'|'history'` × selected owner-wide readSet** before enabling context search. Production v18 prebuilds the two Memory-only projections; a deliberate v23+ rollout builds mixed projections before switching types. No request silently downgrades an unsupported Card capability to Memory-only.

The cron entry renews an **owned supervised background maintenance pump**, separate from HTTP read requests. The host configures its cumulative `maxItems`/`maxMs` budgets per minute plus each refresh's existing ≤1,024/≤5,000-ms quantum; repeated quanta round-robin both intents and active selected projections. It observes epochs at intervals **≤5 s** while explicit service authority and total budget are active; every quantum is separately bounded and no HTTP request credential authorizes the pump. Cron renewal hands off without a gap or duplicate projection writer; idle checks are explicit maintenance work, never lookup triggers. The host fixes cumulative quotas, renewal/overlap policy and Worker capacity before measurement; budget exhaustion is visible and cannot reset counters by restarting a handle. Stop/reschedule on failure with durable progress and verified supervision; a detached OS task/log is insufficient.

A single quantum once every 60 s cannot establish 30 s p95 freshness. The supported cron integration must therefore run/supervise work between ticks, with enough budget to pass **new Memory/Card searchable ≤30 s p95 under continuous writes** and **≤1% not-ready after initial build**. If the product's HTTP hosting cannot own that pump, it needs an explicitly owned maintenance service; those gates remain unmet until that exists. Do not weaken fences, hide unavailable outcomes behind fallback, or move work onto a read to pass. The supervisor owns pump shutdown and failed-opening cleanup. Reader startup/requests never start or renew it.

For `index_unavailable`, the product **reauthenticates and reauthorizes** through its existing Card search plus `recall_memory` gates and marks the overall result **partial**, without new user-facing copy. A captured owner ID/request credential is not fallback authentication. Keep the engine outcome/not-ready counter even if fallback returns useful text; fallback is not a successful engine retrieval or complete owner coverage. Authority/lifecycle failures must not be converted to an authorized empty/partial response.

Maintenance success retains `{status,progress,coverage,channels}` with `status:'rebuilding'|'partial'|'complete'`. Completion refers to the fresh post-commit snapshot defined in §6.1.2, not sidecar commit time or a promise of future readiness. A certified but already stale generation yields rebuilding with unavailable/stale coverage. Timeout/abort may occur after a durable checkpoint/publication, so callers inspect status on a later explicit call rather than assuming rollback of all previous progress.

### 6.3 Embeddings and shared accounting

The default product profile is **lexical-only**. `semantic` remains opt-in and false by default, and the embedder remains configurable but off/unconstructed by default. Enabling semantic search is pending the owner's decision. A configured provider/key alone does not enable it. An explicitly enabled semantic read may make the existing **one query embedding** after complete-coverage/capacity preflight, with guarded transmission and declared budgets; it never embeds documents, downloads a model, starts catch-up or hides a network call inside lexical lookup/fetch. Continuations, evidence-only calls and incomplete semantic coverage make zero embedding calls.

**Hosted embeddings send query text off the machine during semantic reads and document text off the machine during explicit embedding refresh.** They require explicit provider configuration, owner approval, consent and product privacy copy before use; lexical-only launch grants none of these. Local-provider selection remains subject to frozen §20 and explicit configuration. No hosted/local model, network or key operation is part of this authoring revision or the required lexical measurement protocol.

Maintenance embedding configuration and budgets are explicit and separate from reader work budgets. They constrain, rather than replace, the frozen process-wide identity ledger and local child ownership. Provide one shared coordinator across Worker isolates/handles, covering query and refresh attempts, money where applicable and active native children. Provider execution, estimation callbacks and child supervision also stay off the host main thread; the coordinator is an owned supervised service, not disposable request-Worker state. Worker restarts cannot reset totals, double-spend or duplicate local children. Reserve before transmission, revalidate the immutable request/service-grant binding and deadline after reservation under §4.1's two-sample model, retain unknown-usage charges, and keep active capacity reserved until actual underlying settlement/reaping. If that coordination is unavailable, refuse semantic configuration rather than advertising isolation with multiplied budgets. Follow §20's independent child supervisor and quarantine rules when the optional local model is selected.

## 7. WAL and lock policy — C-4

Support canonical DELETE and WAL databases and product sidecars in DELETE and WAL, including read-only context handles. Test all canonical/sidecar combinations; primary performance cells include DELETE/DELETE and WAL/WAL. A background maintainer may explicitly establish its sidecar's configured journal mode during initialization, with no concurrent product handles; it never switches a live canonical mode. Concurrent external journal-mode changes are outside the connection contract.

**Must qualify:** Linux ext4 on the production volume, private directory mode **0700**, canonical/sidecar/companion files **0600**. **Should qualify:** WSL2 ext4 and macOS APFS, reported separately; neither substitutes for the the production host result. Network filesystems are out of deployment scope. The read-only reader is explicitly permitted to let SQLite create owner-only WAL/SHM companions and read marks in the protected directory. Safe identity/permission checks still apply to existing and newly created companions. Production remains **DELETE**; WAL rollout awaits the owner after the product's re-run on `546d57ac`, and P2 does not perform that switch.

Reuse `topicJournalMode`, `createTopicSqlitePolicy`, native diagnostics and the Worker dispatch policy; generalize their use rather than adding another sleep/retry subsystem. Detect each database from the complete safe 100-byte format-3 header, verified descriptor/device/inode, before lock-taking metadata PRAGMAs. Only 2/2 enables WAL waiting. Unknown/truncated headers retain zero wait and normal layout refusal. Install the policy **before the first configuration/validation statement**, including `application_id`/`user_version`, to cover E1-C2's first-statement race.

Use one operation-wide native WAL wait window of **100 ms**, capped by remaining monotonic request/maintenance deadline. Canonical and sidecar connections share its start and end; do not restart it per connection, transaction or statement. Query work consumes this window. Refresh floored nonnegative remaining time before prepare, cached execution, iterator step, BEGIN/COMMIT/ROLLBACK, including zero after expiry. DELETE connections retain zero wait. All waits happen in the Worker. No application transaction retry, polling sleep, parent-thread wait, hidden five-second native timeout or automatic repeated request is allowed.

Canonical/sidecar reads use the frozen deferred-BEGIN/query_only transaction discipline; maintenance's sidecar writes use separate bounded transactions. Never hold a canonical snapshot while awaiting a provider, callback or next quantum. Background maintenance must release read snapshots between bounded phases so it does not indefinitely pin the WAL. Public cancellation settles independently; native rollback/close and WAL lock release may lag until termination is observed.

Replace blanket WAL/header/companion refusal **only for the product profile** with safe live-WAL handling. Allow SQLite's normal coordinated WAL/SHM access, including safe companion creation when needed and permitted by the explicitly opted-in deployment. Do not use `immutable`, force checkpoints, restore SHM bytes, manually unlink a live WAL/SHM, recover canonical hot journals, open canonical writable or change its journal mode. Validate existing companion identity/type/protection and safe creation context; unsafe/symlinked companions still refuse. Unsupported filesystem/SQLite read-only WAL arrangements report `index_unavailable` honestly, never fall back to canonical writes. Supported arrangements must be qualified on the target runtime/platform.

Quiescent canonical schema/data/user_version/journal mode stay unchanged. For DELETE, demand canonical file-hash equality. For WAL, separate logical/canonical preservation from native SHM read marks/creation: reuse the native-control comparison, cold differences limited to permitted read marks and warmed equality described in `docs/plans/topic-overview-member-pages.md:777`. Do not promise whole-file equality during concurrent writes or erase legitimate SHM changes to satisfy a hash check. Legacy sidecar bytes remain untouched by product maintenance.

Native budget exhaustion remains `storage_busy`; parent deadline cutoff is `read_timeout`. Never reclassify native busy as unavailable/unsupported/timeout just to pass a gate. Optional native diagnostics use the existing closed E1-C2 stage/statement families and numeric primary/extended codes, adding fixed context statement-family labels only through an audited enum extension. No SQL, native messages, paths, identifiers, text or credentials appear. Diagnostics do not enter metered text.

Cancellation/native cleanup must not hold an ordinary writer through a supervisor lock or retained resource attribution. Any SQLite read-lock disposal lag must remain within existing production-configured writer busy/operation bounds, with no reader-induced deadline overrun. Record the actual capture/review deadline values and public-write/close intervals; do not raise them for the benchmark or infer lock release from public timeout settlement. Validate write progress while a canceled native reader is still being reaped.

**WAL `storage_busy = 0` is a measured product gate for the registered workload, not an assertion that arbitrary external locks cannot produce busy.** Deterministic exclusive-lock/exhaustion tests must still prove truthful busy classification. There is no inspected-code reason to drop the product's gate; the first-statement race has reusable mitigation, but P2 has not yet been measured.

## 8. Errors, evidence and schema requirements

### 8.1 Error contract

All data errors use `{ok:false,error:{code,retryable,...sanitizedDetails}}`. Preserve existing codes and precedence; only `storage_busy` is automatically marked retryable. There are no automatic retries. New lifecycle errors are confined to the new constructors/handles/profile.

| Code | Product meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `invalid_input`, `invalid_read_set` | Bad strict grammar, unsupported option/type or invalid/fixed-set mismatch. |
| `authority_required` | Missing/denied request/service authority at entry; published revocation/erasure or optional ownerGeneration mismatch; reused/wrong-request credential or request credential supplied to maintenance. No index/evidence access or data. |
| `scope_changed` | The second synchronous currentness check immediately before final canonical reading, or before building a data-bearing envelope on a path without that read, observes changed/denied/unverifiable request/service state, including ownerGeneration mismatch. Bare error, no private diagnostics/progress/coverage/counts. A change published only after that check may leave the operation able to finish before its cutoff; no final-read synchronization is claimed. |
| `invalid_cursor`, `cursor_stale` | Invalid binding versus changed/expired/evicted/Worker-lost ranking; never rerank silently. |
| `invalid_snapshot`, `snapshot_stale` | Invalid snapshot scope/integrity versus changed generations/full item fences or compact fence mismatch, subject to frozen eligibility/revision precedence. |
| `revision_conflict`, `evidence_unavailable` | Existing exact-revision/eligibility errors; whole fetch refuses, never substitutes a new revision. |
| `index_unavailable` | No ready matching validated projection, unsafe/unusable index or unsupported WAL arrangement; existing coverage/channels/trace only after the final currentness sample and before the parent cutoff. |
| `token_count_unavailable`, `context_item_too_large` | Existing exact-counter/size refusal; no heuristic, truncation or partially fetched evidence. |
| `storage_busy`, `storage_error` | Native contention versus other sanitized storage failure; retain stage/numeric diagnostics when opted in. |
| `read_timeout` | New handle's hard response cutoff, nonretryable; late results discarded. |
| `read_cancelled` | New handle's explicit AbortSignal cancellation, nonretryable; late results discarded. |
| `read_in_progress` | New handle occupied, or process-wide store/resource-domain quarantine blocks replacement, including after a constructor failure; no queue. |
| `reader_failed`, `store_closed` | Unexpected Worker/protocol/cleanup failure versus explicit closed/discarded handle. |
| Existing opener codes | Preserve `invalid_path`, `database_not_found`, `unsafe_database_file`, `unsupported_database` and their existing safe precedence. |

Scope/store/authority failures suppress private diagnostics. Candidate failures may retain existing `details:{channels,coverage,trace}`; assembly candidate failures may retain `details:{candidates}` and the existing `_meta` extraction **only under §4.1.2's two-sample data rule**. Coverage, private counts, trace, progress and nested candidate diagnostics are data even on `ok:false`; an early index failure takes its final currentness sample immediately before building that error envelope. Failed final sampling replaces it with bare `scope_changed` and strips every private field, including `_meta`; an already latched parent timeout/cancel/close takes precedence and also strips them. If there is no final sample, return only a bare sanitized error code/retryable flag, never private diagnostics. An earlier successful final-read sample is reused without any post-read callback. Parent cutoff checks cover these errors just as they cover successful and partial results. Worker lifecycle failures carry no invented counts or partial data; supervisor-only cleanup metadata/native telemetry retains §4.4–§4.5's separate sanitized channel. A malformed/future schema remains an opener refusal, not empty results. An unsupported requested capability on an otherwise supported layout is `invalid_input`.

### 8.2 Evidence, refs and model boundary

U5a §13.6, U5b §§15.5–15.8 and applicable §§17–§20 remain normative. Preserve `representation-v2` serializer byte order, exact counter budget, whole bodies/source units, omission algebra, default omission, trust framing and `untrusted-data-not-instructions`. Snapshot and diagnostics stay outside assembly metered text in the existing two `_meta` keys. The host tests that neither is forwarded to the model; no extra text block or `structuredContent` is added.

Compact refs retain `ns/type/id/rev/bodyRev/f`, eight-character mandatory `f`, core verification in both preparatory/final reads, and immutable canonical namespace mapping. They confer no authority. Full refs retain their existing semantics; raw `fences` remains unavailable to tool callers. Host-only snapshots bind exactly the delivered ordered set, full fences and generations. Preserve the frozen documented difference: a parent-only filed→filed MOC move can leave compact-ref fetch unchanged while snapshot fetch is stale. Do not collapse all mutations to `snapshot_stale`; eligibility and revision errors retain §15.8.3 precedence.

### 8.3 Per-capability schema table

Physical layout and consumed tables/indexes must validate, not merely a relabeled `user_version`. The supported-version allowlist is explicit and does not automatically admit a future version.

| Capability | Canonical requirement under P2 | Sidecar / deployment requirement | Backup / restore | Required time/size outputs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Memory candidates, exact Memory evidence, Memory assembly (`current`/`history`) | **v18**, also accepted physical layouts v20–v33; no v19, below-v18 or unknown future layout. Never query absent Card tables. | Search/assembly need a certified ready product projection; assembly needs an exact counter. Exact evidence works without a sidecar and still checks refs/snapshot fences. | Back up canonical consistently; rebuild sidecar under restore quiescence, invalidate old request bindings and authenticate new requests before exposure. | `backupMs`, `restoreCanonicalVerifyMs`, `sidecarRebuildMs`, `sidecarVerifyMs`, `freshReaderVerifyMs`; report all, no invented time gate. |
| Card candidates, exact Card evidence, mixed assembly | **v23–v33**. v18/v20–v22 expose Memory only; Cards refuse `invalid_input`. Product floor is stricter than baseline table detection. | Same certified search/assembly rule; canonical exact evidence needs no index. Preserve eligibility/support/current dependencies. | Canonical consistent backup includes Card/support data; rebuild mixed current/history projections. | Same times; sidecar main/WAL/SHM/total/peak bytes at **1,112 and 3,336 Memories with registered Cards**. |
| Background lexical build/catch-up | v18 for Memory, v23+ for Cards, within explicit allowlist. | Canonical read-only; explicit sidecar maintenance authority and maxItems/maxMs. | No request-time rebuild; supervised rebuild before enabling the restored path. | Initial build/catch-up/verification duration and durable progress, including cumulative quanta. |
| Semantic query / explicit vector catch-up | No extra schema beyond selected types; off by default, owner decision pending. | Explicit provider/configuration/consent, identity/full coverage/shared budgets; local pack rules remain §20. | Default backup omits derived vectors; do not regenerate them automatically. Rebuild only with separate explicit vector budget/consent. | Report extra vector bytes and explicit refresh/verification time separately if enabled; no launch inference run. |
| WAL readers/maintenance | Same schema gates; never upgrade v18 for WAL. | Qualified production Linux ext4 required; WSL2 ext4/APFS separately reported; Worker-only bounded policy. | SQLite-consistent canonical backup including committed WAL state; do not copy only a live main file or replay copied companions manually. | Backup and canonical verification time per journal mode; qualified read-mark controls and cleanup/lock-release lag. |
| Later optional features | P2 does not enable Raw search, assets, roles or MOC/relations retrieval. Mandatory v33 current-dependent data is honored. | Additional retrieval composition requires separate explicit opt-in/audit under §§17–§20. | Follow each feature's existing canonical backup requirements; sidecar is never canonical evidence. | Report-only feature-specific costs before any added rollout. |

### 8.4 Backup, restore and verification

**Recommend and define the default: back up canonical storage consistently and rebuild the product sidecar; do not include the sidecar in the product backup.** It is a derived cache, with independent format, file-identity bindings and possibly provider-derived vectors. The product's names and separate sidecar are approved as versioned opt-ins; this choice does not authorize a canonical schema/mode change. Backup must use the product's supported SQLite-consistent procedure or a quiescent closed database, preserving committed state in DELETE/WAL. Raw live main-file copying is insufficient for WAL.

On restore, validate physical canonical layout/store identity/logical content and schema without migrating, invalidate every old request/handle binding, and discard copied product publication pointers/certificates. Keep admissions closed and clients/old readers/maintenance stopped, with confirmed supervisor drain before re-exposure, as required by §4.1.3. Build the configured Memory-only or mixed current/history owner projections in the supervised background path with separate service authority; search is unavailable until publication and may use the reauthenticated partial fallback only after restore exposure gates allow requests. Canonical exact evidence remains subject to new request credentials and snapshot/ref checks. Optional ownerGeneration is part of the current host state, never revived from an old credential; a fresh mandatory per-namespace registry/realm epoch is not introduced by v1.1.

An operator may archive a sidecar separately for diagnostics, marked **non-restorable cache evidence**; it is not the recommended backup and is never accepted as ready after copying. Any future supported sidecar-backup/restore fast path needs its own consistent-pair protocol and audit. Hosted vectors are not silently reconstructed during restore.

Record `backupMs` (consistent-backup start to durable completion), `restoreCanonicalVerifyMs` (restored canonical validation only), `sidecarRebuildMs` (all explicit lexical quanta to publication), `sidecarVerifyMs` (deep validation component) and `freshReaderVerifyMs` (bounded per-call publication/canonical checks). Include backup bytes, restored bytes, sidecar main/WAL/SHM bytes and total/peak temporary disk usage. Freeze fixture Card counts and both world sizes before collecting these **report-only** times/sizes; no numerical backup/verify target was supplied. These are separate from request-latency gates, which still include their own bounded verification and awaited close.

## 9. Deterministic acceptance before performance claims

These are future implementation checks, **not run in this docs-only task**. Ordinary suites assert functional outcomes with mock clocks/rendezvous points, not elapsed timing, overlap counts or p95 gates. Use Node **22.16.0 and 24.15.0**, physical v18/v23/current-v33 fixtures, DELETE and WAL, and intermediate accepted-version smoke coverage. Do not edit another owner's tests/fixtures to make P2 pass.

| ID | Required observable |
| --- | --- |
| P2-01 Defaults and versioning | Preserve legacy goldens/defaults without v1.1 selection. Explicitly distinguish frozen v1 from v1.1 profiles, sidecars, certificate/HMAC domains and opaque tokens; no silent reinterpretation/conversion. Data DTO version/representation/serializer bytes stay unchanged. The new constructor requires the literal v1.1 profile. |
| P2-02 Real execution isolation | Exercise actual exported handle and installed Worker. SQLite, tokenization, canonical validation and exact counter execute in Worker, including cold initialization and fetch. The parent owns only bounded input/transport/control work. No static SQLite link leaks into local transport/host/CLI closures. |
| P2-03 Cutoff and cancel | Controlled Worker/native-boundary stalls plus mock parent clock: expiry/abort settles before an intentionally delayed termination acknowledgement; no late success, slot reuse, publication callback or leaked unhandled rejection. Cover pre-abort, abort during fetch/count/final read, close races, crash, idle expiry, cleanup failure and simultaneous requests. Assert termination/child cleanup separately from public settlement; real ≤50 ms overshoot is an opt-in measurement gate. |
| P2-04 v18 preservation | Use a real old-pin physical v18 file. Build/catch up only the sidecar, then candidates/fetch/assembly Memory reads. Compare canonical DDL, user_version, logical hash and DELETE file hash before/after; old pin reopens. Under WAL apply the native-control SHM qualification and old-pin reopen on supported arrangements. No canonical migrate/open-write call occurs; Card requests refuse without querying absent tables. |
| P2-05 Schema/capabilities | Physical v23 and current mixed Memory/Card success; v20–v22 remain Memory-only on P2; v19/future/malformed layouts refuse untouched. Verify capability table and no implicit feature enablement. |
| P2-06 ReadSet/authority | One, four and 32 selected namespaces succeed; raw selected list of 33, duplicates, mixed owners and malformed/accessor input refuse before storage. An authorized inventory of personal+32 projects instead selects personal+31 by recent-projects-v1 and visibly reports partial/counts. Equal/missing activity, reordered inventory and selection revision changes have independent known answers. Workspace ACL filters inventory before selection; deny at entry/final samples under the request model. Permutations preserve mapping/results; no unauthorized subset or omitted-project statistics influence ranks/text. |
| P2-07 Independent ranking oracle | Freeze bilingual/mixed and Japanese fixtures with relevant Memory/Card matches in all four namespaces. Independently calculate pooled N/df/BM25, global channel ranks, RRF and canonical tie order; compare exact identities/ranks. Include lexical-only, complete semantic, vector gaps, score ties, equal bodies, both intents and readSet permutations. Assert one query embedding, no namespace truncation/quota and no host RRF. Preserve the known single-Han miss. |
| P2-08 Ready-only reads | Trap all create/write/reconcile/refresh/repair/deep-index-scan paths and document embeddings during reads. Missing/building/stale/corrupt/unvalidated states return honest unavailable; empty matches only on ready exhausted lexical search. Exact evidence remains usable with no sidecar. Reader and maintainer can run independently. |
| P2-09 Maintenance durability | Rendezvous at checkpoint/publication and provider awaits; insert/correct/delete/revoke/change eligibility/dependencies from an independent writer. No incomplete statistics or wrong-revision vectors are certified; a certified captured generation may become stale at the publication race, but is never accepted as ready (§6.1.2). Interrupted uncommitted batches replay; committed checkpoints survive reopen. Catch-up ignores unchanged candidate texts. Cover owner-wide projection keys and absent prebuilt combinations. |
| P2-10 WAL correctness | Test canonical/sidecar DELETE/WAL cross-product, no-companion checkpointed WAL, live companions, first-statement contention during fresh writer close, safe-file replacement and unsafe companions. Assert one shared native wait window, configured before first statement, zero DELETE wait, refreshed rollback/iterator policy, truthful busy after exhaustion, no retries/migration/mode change/immutable/manual companion deletion. Use rendezvous for outcomes. |
| P2-11 Frozen evidence semantics | Extend §§15.5–15.8 known-answer serialization and mutation matrix to four namespaces and IPC. Snapshot fetch returns exactly admitted items/order; compact refs round-trip against fixed mapping. Receipt deletion, revision changes, eligibility loss and parent-only moves keep their exact frozen distinctions. No callback/await follows worker final reading; no unvalidated partial fetch/assembly escapes timeout. |
| P2-12 Provider ownership | Fake providers test zero calls for lexical/fetch/continuation/gaps and maintenance lexical mode; explicit semantic preflight/reservation/guard only. Multiple reader/maintainer Workers and restarts share totals and active local-child slots; timeout cannot free capacity before actual settlement. No real network/key/model operation. |
| P2-13 Installed artifact | Offline archive install, actual exported reader/maintainer/Worker/counter/bootstrap dependencies, no source fallback. Mechanically derive archive/runtime/Worker/doc closure and independently installed closure, diff and print counts; manifest coverage and exact shipped bytes required. Check child exit/cleanup and sanitized diagnostics. |
| P2-14 Request revocation boundaries (v1.1) | Publish revoke or erasure denial before the entry sample: authority_required, no index/statistics/evidence/data. Pause after successful entry, publish changed credential/erasure state, resume before the final sample: scope_changed and no data. Publish after the final canonical read: the request may finish before its unchanged cutoff, with no post-read callback. Also publish between the final currentness sample and snapshot acquisition: bounded in-flight completion is permitted; never assert a protected interval. Advance the mock clock past the cutoff and verify no late data, even when currentness passed. Exercise candidates, assembly, fetch and status. |
| P2-15 Publication handoff (F2, v1.1 access binding) | Maintain/certify with service authority, close all handles, and use a separately minted fresh HTTP credential to obtain correct candidates/assembly/status without maintenance/deep scan. Different request IDs/issuedAt/handle incarnations must not change accessDigest. Independently corrupt/miss MAC, pointer, file/schema identity, projection or access/canonical/checkpoint bindings; refuse/stale exactly. Optional ownerGeneration change makes an old certificate access-stale only after a new valid credential passes admission. Crash/publication atomicity and unchanged vector publication remain pinned. |
| P2-16 Failed-opening ownership (F3) | Mock opening timeout while native work remains live; public rejection has cleanupId and supervisor shows pending. A new constructor through another supervisor cannot bypass process-wide quarantine, including with a new request credential. Bootstrap throws after acquiring canonical/sidecar/outer resource-attribution/provider resources; supervisor close/drain owns them without a returned handle. Confirm release only after native disposal, terminal cleanup failure latch and telemetry coverage; no double close or invisible child. |
| P2-17 BUSY/cutoff race (F5) | Rendezvous native wrapper returning primary 5/6 immediately before cutoff, block retrieval postMessage until after timeout, and verify committed shared failure still appears on supervisor drain. Separate termination during an unreturned native call gives incomplete telemetry and invalid zero-busy coverage. Cover constructor failures, ring overflow/missing sequence, record-before-mapping and complete clean drain. |
| P2-18 Closed status goldens (F6) | Independently construct exact full DTOs for every lexical/semantic state/reason, selected/omitted scope, nullability/count and precedence combination. Crash/reopen during initial/delta work preserves durable building/catching_up status with no active-execution claim. Closed handle returns error only; fresh ready reads consume record counts without COUNT/deep validation. |
| P2-19 Protocol population/exposure (F7) | With fabricated numeric attempts, independently derive nearest-rank successful-only and all-attempt statistics, 3 warm-ups, 50/10 minimums, 500/100 ceilings and inconclusive outcome; fast failures cannot count. Retained control needs write overlap/commits and no fresh-close count, fresh writer needs ten close overlaps, baseline needs commits only. No real timing gate is run by this ordinary functional check. |
| P2-20 Restore, quiescence and fallback | Canonical-only v18/v23 DELETE/WAL backup/restore keeps admissions closed, stops clients/readers/maintenance, confirms native drain, invalidates old request bindings and explicitly rebuilds the sidecar before search exposure; copied certificates cannot become ready. A simulated active-client/undrained erasure precondition refuses destructive work at the product boundary. Exact evidence remains independently authorized without an index. On index_unavailable, fallback reauthenticates/reauthorizes and marks partial; deny a now-revoked identity instead of reusing its captured owner ID. Keep failure counters/no new UI copy/no implicit maintenance. Timing remains opt-in. |
| P2-21 Publication/currentness race (R2-1) | Under WAL, pause after the last maintenance pre-commit canonical/dependency check sees E; commit an ordinary writer mutation to E+1; resume sidecar COMMIT. Assert E's complete certificate/pointer is durably installed with the original correct digest, while a **new** post-commit canonical snapshot makes refresh return rebuilding/unavailable/stale with null current counts. Fresh status/read must not report ready or return E candidates. Explicit E+1 catch-up restores readiness. Separately commit after the post-commit snapshot: completion at E is valid, later reads are stale. Cover no-op refresh, timeout after publication and pointer/certificate atomicity without claiming cross-database atomicity. |
| P2-22 Request credential isolation and service separation (v1.1) | Mint for one authenticated HTTP request/handle, then present the same credential in a second request or independently opened handle: authority_required before index access, even if owner/readSet/issuedAt are identical. An opaque binding must distinguish those lifetimes; changing a caller flag cannot reclassify it. The maintainer refuses that request credential at construction/use with authority_required; a legitimate independently authorized service credential can run later bounded quanta without borrowing any HTTP identity. Closing/disconnecting a request prevents new reads; its cleanup records confer no authority. |
| P2-23 Writer native provenance/drain (R2-3) | Install writer observation before ordinary open; inject a native BUSY in open, write and rollback/close paths, including a public mapper that emits a generic error with no native code. Native primary 5/6 remains in the parent ledger and fails the designated zero-busy comparison. Test lost chunk/seal, wrong epoch, duplicate/resend, overflow, missing operation, crash before flush and final close outside the write interval; incomplete writer coverage invalidates the cell even with complete reader telemetry. Use fake native outcomes/rendezvous for deterministic cases; no real timing gate in ordinary tests. |
| P2-24 Supervisor retention (R2-4) | Complete at least 10,000 synthetic fresh-request operations across repeated supervisor instances, with deterministic registry counters and mock time. Assert completed count/metadata/diagnostic caps, oldest-first byte/count eviction, fixed five-minute expiry without renewal, shared drain delivery, early acknowledgement and expired-ID errors. Keep pending and failed quarantines across every eviction/clock advance and confirm admission refusal rather than safety-record eviction; only verified disposal/recovery releases them. Closed-owner indexes and ID tombstones cannot grow with request count. Eviction before diagnostic consumption makes the corresponding comparison incomplete/invalid, not zero-busy. |
| P2-25 Optional ownerGeneration (v1.1) | With generation present, mismatch at entry returns authority_required with no data; change after entry and before the final sample returns scope_changed. Positive equality at both samples succeeds subject to all canonical fences/cutoffs. Zero/negative/noninteger or unauthenticated field removal is refused. With generation omitted by configuration, normal credential-revocation and erasure-denial checks still work; no namespace authority table/vector or synthesized generation is accessed. Ordinary content commits do not spuriously revoke authority. |
| P2-26 Synchronous adapter/checklist (v1.1) | For each data-bearing success/error/status envelope record exactly two Worker-local isCurrent samples: entry after bootstrap before private/index access, and final immediately before canonical reading or, on a path without that read, immediately before building its data-bearing envelope. Entry refusal stops after one; a bare error without any private fields may omit the second. No third sample after final reading, even when it throws. Verify no callback/parent RPC/network/await inside or after final reading; throwing/unknown/Promise-returning currentness fails closed with entry/final codes. Bind disconnect to public abort, preserve lifecycle errors/drain ownership, and verify service authority is separate. Product integration checks the six-item adapter checklist, parent-visible cutoff/expiry handoff and stopped-client erasure precondition; no asynchronous auth is invoked inside the Worker. |
| P2-27 Diagnostic errors and revocation (revision 4) | For both findCandidates and contextRead, pass entry, collect private coverage/counts/trace/candidate diagnostics, then rendezvous an early index failure **without a final canonical read**. Publish credential revocation/erasure denial or change ownerGeneration before constructing the error: second sample returns false and the result is bare scope_changed, with no details, progress, coverage, counts, candidate diagnostics, trace or `_meta` at any nesting level. Unchanged authority permits the exact existing diagnostic error shape after two samples; no extra private work/await follows the final-envelope sample. Omitting that sample must strip all private fields; entry-only diagnostic delivery fails acceptance. Also test a passed final-read sample followed by a canonical error: no third/post-read callback, and only otherwise permitted diagnostics before cutoff. Test service progress/error envelopes and parent timeout/cancel/close races separately, preserving their precedence and independent sanitized native telemetry. |
| P2-28 Trusted deadline handoff (revision 4) | Mock parent/Worker clocks and trusted issuance: assert parent admission capture precedes validation/open, exact control-record shape/formulas and identity binding, clock domain, converted expiry and watchdog arm before dispatch. Block Worker bootstrap/native work before it can report expiry; the parent still expires at the earlier known bound. Reject malformed/overflowing, unknown-domain, wrong-request/handle/credential/epoch and inconsistent/Worker-only expiry records. Independently advance UTC or Worker-relative clocks without changing the retained monotonic cutoff; include UTC issuance differing from admission and delayed UTC sampling (conservative conversion). A new process/domain cannot resume old control. Already expired issuance is authority_required; expiry after admission times out opening/read without Worker cooperation. All checks use mock time, not elapsed settlement assertions. |
| P2-29 Cumulative request cutoff (revision 4) | With admission at monotonic 0 and a 5,000-ms request, make successful open consume 4,900 ms, then call fetch with deadlineMs=5,000: the retained operation cutoff is 5,000 ms, **only 100 ms remains**, and receipt at 5,000 ms is read_timeout. Across second calls, candidate continuation/page boundaries, cursor/dispatch replay and any otherwise allowed Worker restart, assert the original admission/request/credential cutoffs never renew; wrong-request replay is refused, duplicate/old-epoch results are discarded and quarantine remains enforced. Supply credential expiry converted to 4,950 ms: fetch at 4,900 ms receives only 50 ms despite its longer method/request limits. Exercise full/partial assembly, size-limited candidate pages, status/progress and candidate/assembly diagnostic errors: results fully computed/validated before cutoff but held at a message rendezvous until at/after it must deliver only read_timeout, with no private payload or `_meta`. Cover both request-limited and expiry-limited cutoffs, even when the timer callback is delayed; next calls after cutoff dispatch nothing. Native telemetry/drain still completes independently. Mock clocks/rendezvous only; real ≤50 ms public settlement stays in §10's opt-in measurement. |

Implementation verification must follow the dispatch traps: primary runs the CI-equivalent acceptance matrix including both SQLite-free import checks; no broad sandbox suites. Record actual build/typecheck/test exit codes, with pipefail or explicit status capture. Cross-check changed-file and artifact inventories by independent routes, include untracked whitespace, and report counts. The primary adds this document to `packaging/artifact-files.json` if it ships; this one-file authoring packet deliberately cannot edit the manifest, following the same docs-only boundary recorded in `docs/plans/topic-overview-member-pages.md:784`.

## 10. Opt-in measurement protocol and pre-registered gates

Propose **`measure:context-read-product`, protocol `P2-product-v3`**. This revises the unimplemented v1/v2 plans to require independent writer diagnostic coverage and bounded-history consumption; no harness or result is claimed. Reuse E1-C2's independent writer/control/overlap machinery and the **successful-population rules of frozen `topic-member-previews-v1`**, inspected in its companion plan at commit `0a24f66c5726` (same sample/count/quantile/lifecycle rules restated here so acceptance does not depend on another checkout). Timing/contention assertions belong only in the opt-in path, serially scheduled by the primary's machine-wide queue, potentially a future CI `extra` job. No network, keys, real user data or real inference is required.

Before any run, record the implementation/profile/protocol commits, expanded cell inventory and count, corpus/label/query hashes, exact production Node 24 patch version, counter identity, hardware/filesystem, load, normal operation deadlines, pump cadence/cumulative budgets, writer rates, reader/writer diagnostic capacities/coverage protocols, completed-history drain/ack schedule and all gates. Node 22.16.0/24.15.0 remain compatibility checks; record and run the deployed Node 24 patch on the production host for launch rather than inferring production acceptance from another runtime. The product did not supply its patch in this answer; obtain it before registration. No post-result threshold, sampling, query, wait-window or budget tuning; a change creates a new protocol revision and rerun.

The product identifies a private, unmerged spike fixture in its own repository (a corpus recipe, a seeding script and a spike report). It is a synthetic smoke set, not a quality benchmark, and it was not inspected here.

### 10.1 Cells, populations and timing intervals

- **Worlds/types:** initial 1,112 and 3,336 Memories over personal plus three projects. Physical v18 Memory-only compatibility cells; physical v23 and current v33 **owner-wide Memory+Card launch cells**, both current/history, registered Card counts. Record initial/final counts during inserts. Full four-namespace selected coverage is required in launch cells; separately test overflow partial coverage.
- **Methods/languages:** zh, ja and mixed queries, separately labeled per cell. Candidates default limit 12 and max-24 stress; assembly real exact counter, default 1,500 tokens/max12 and separately max4,000/max24 stress. Fetch includes 1 and 24 refs, full/compact adapter and host snapshot. All ≤24-ref fetch profiles carry the fetch gates. Do not pool languages, methods, worlds, schemas, modes or load conditions into one percentile.
- **Modes/platforms:** DELETE/DELETE and WAL/WAL required, with mixed modes as separately registered controls. production Linux ext4 is launch-required; WSL2 ext4/APFS are separate qualification reports. No automatic production DELETE→WAL switch follows a benchmark.
- **Reader lifecycle:** **fresh public open → await one read → await close in finally per HTTP attempt**. Time immediately before constructor entry through awaited cleanup completion, including bootstrap, Worker startup, validation, cloning/IPC, delivery and close. For constructor failure, the all-attempt lifecycle ends only after `supervisor.drain(error.cleanupId)`; report public rejection separately. No hidden pre-open/priming call in a fresh sample. Record public read-only call and final-native/resource/close components separately. The warm candidate reference has a retained initialized handle and ready index; its explicit 300 ms read-call gate is an additional product requirement, not a substitute for the fresh lifecycle gate. Other retained/continuation observations are report-only for latency.
- **Sample rules:** exactly **3 fresh-handle warm-up requests** per main-series cell, reported separately; then collect **≥50 successful measured requests**, without selecting by speed, at most **500 attempts**. Collect an additional **≥10 successful cold runs**, at most **100 cold attempts**, each from a fresh Node process or a documented equivalent unprimed public Worker topology; no warm-ups inside cold samples. Report outer process launch separately, time the same public open/read/await-close lifecycle, and never mix cold with the main series. Warm candidate reference uses the same success minimum/ceiling in its own labeled series. A ceiling reached below the success minimum is **inconclusive, not accepted**.
- **Gated successful population:** lifecycle p95/max gates apply only to `ok:true` engine results that returned validated candidates, assembly or a complete evidence fetch, with no fallback/error masquerading as success. Deliberate scope-partial overflow results may be successful only in their registered overflow series, never the full-owner launch series. An empty result is successful only when the independent fixture expects a complete selected-set empty result. There is no P2 progressing-incomplete-index success. Keep the full all-attempt numeric/status/coverage record including failures, drain, warm-ups and cold runs; report its p50/p95/max too. Fast unavailable/busy/timeout/cancel responses cannot lower gated percentiles or supply missing successes. No silent retry, slow-sample removal or pooling retries into one fast success; report retry-inclusive user latency separately.
- **Quantiles/deadlines:** nearest-rank p50/p95 at sorted ranks `ceil(.50*n)` / `ceil(.95*n)`; report maximum, counts and count of host-loop gaps strictly >100 ms. Use unchanged production-configured normal deadlines, sufficient to observe the stated latency maxima; do not terminate slow normal calls early and count only the survivors. Cutoff/abort injection is a separate expected-failure series; public cutoff semantics still gate every normal attempt. All sizes inherit unbracketed product targets; the sole supplied size-specific difference is fresh candidate p95 700/1,000 ms.
- **Writers:** independent process, production capture/review operation types separately, fresh open/write/close approximately every **20 ms while reads are active**, with no saturated backlog/keeper for fresh-writer cells. Cover same-owner/same-projection and other-owner writers separately; a stable other-project control does not replace the mutating projection. Log actual public-write start/end, commit outcome, per-write close and production deadline. Each concurrent main/cold measured request needs actual **read-call/public-write-call overlap**; open/close-only overlap, PID life and sleeps do not qualify. Retain missed-overlap attempts as invalid for that concurrency claim, and collect replacements without speed selection. Require ≥50 successful valid main overlaps and ≥10 successful valid cold overlaps within the same attempt ceilings.
- **Index phases:** separate absent/initial build, ready, continuous same-projection writes with the explicitly scheduled pump, and post-catch-up series. Prebuild current/history before normal availability/latency series. Not-ready tests expect unavailable; launch availability series count every such outcome rather than excluding dirty periods. Observer probes and fallback do not build an index.

### 10.2 Contention exposure classes (audit F7)

Additionally retain E1-C2's **five fixed 30 s windows** per registered contention cell plus paired no-reader writer baseline, reversing pair order on odd repetitions. These fixed-window controls and the success-count latency series are separate populations; meeting one never substitutes for the other. Do not lengthen a fixed window after seeing its overlap count.

| Registered cell class | Required public-write overlap / native commits / close overlap |
| --- | --- |
| Fresh per-request writer reader-load | Per 30 s window: ≥20 reader attempts overlapping actual public-write calls, ≥100 successful native commits inside those calls/window, **≥10 attempts overlapping fresh per-write close**. Also satisfy per-request overlap/success minima in the separate latency series. |
| Retained-writer reader-load control | Per window: ≥20 attempts overlapping actual public-write calls and ≥100 in-call/in-window native commits. **No fresh-close minimum**; final retained shutdown close is reported separately and cannot qualify as per-write close. |
| Paired no-reader writer baseline | ≥100 successful native public-write commits per window; no reader/write-overlap or close-overlap minimum because there are no readers. Record the same writer lifecycle/deadlines. |
| Idle latency/cold or quiescent functional series | No contention-exposure minima. Apply its success minimum and all applicable loop/latency/bytes/functional gates. |
| Deliberate lock-exhaustion, abort or crash series | Assert its pre-registered expected outcome; report overlap/diagnostic completeness. Never include these injected-failure samples as a zero-busy/normal-latency pass. |

Below an applicable exposure minimum makes that fixed-window cell **invalid**, never pass. Baseline commits exclude opener/fixture commits, sleeps and retained shutdown close. Successful latency samples lacking their required overlap remain insufficient even if a separate window has ample commits.

### 10.3 Adopted latency, loop and busy gates

The product's recall budget is about **2 s end to end**, with additional `ask_cairn` model synthesis. The following are engine acceptance gates, not a promise about synthesis latency. Fresh gates time the full lifecycle defined above, including awaited close; fresh cold runs use the same supplied fresh bound. The warm candidate reference alone times the already open handle's read call.

| Method / population | 1,112 Memories + registered Cards | 3,336 Memories + registered Cards |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `findCandidates`, warm successful reference | p95 **≤300 ms**, maximum **≤1,500 ms** | p95 **≤300 ms**, maximum **≤1,500 ms** |
| `findCandidates`, fresh successful main and cold lifecycle | p95 **≤700 ms**, maximum **≤1,500 ms** | p95 **≤1,000 ms**, maximum **≤1,500 ms** |
| `fetchCandidateEvidence`, ≤24 refs, fresh successful main and cold lifecycle | p95 **≤150 ms**, maximum **≤500 ms** | p95 **≤150 ms**, maximum **≤500 ms** |
| `contextRead`, fresh successful main and cold lifecycle | p95 **≤800 ms**, maximum **≤2,000 ms** | p95 **≤800 ms**, maximum **≤2,000 ms** |

| Other metric | Pre-registered gate / output |
| --- | --- |
| Host main loop | **Absolute maximum ≤100 ms per request in DELETE and WAL**, every attempt including failures, warm-ups, fresh/cold startup, timeout settlement, clone/delivery and close/failed-open cleanup callbacks. Count >100 ms must be zero. Track 1 ms heartbeat gaps, event-loop-delay maximum and parent synchronous dispatch/settlement/close sections, bracket through the final drain; keep raw maximum and idle baseline without subtracting noise to claim pass. Writers run outside the host reader loop. |
| Public timeout / cancel | Reject **≤50 ms after absolute deadline**, including opening; AbortSignal settles ≤50 ms after host-observed abort. No success at/after cutoff or after accepted cancellation. Applies to every attempt, not just successful latency population. Report native/resource cleanup lag separately; writer bounds remain unchanged. |
| WAL busy | **Zero public `storage_busy` and zero native primary 5/6** on reader/open/cleanup and concurrent writer operations in every required normal WAL cell/series/drain. Require complete reader/maintainer supervisor telemetry (§4.5) **and** independent-process writer observation/acknowledged final seals (§4.5.1), including writer opening/rollback/close and paired baselines. Native failures count before public mapping. A discarded reader result or generic writer error cannot erase BUSY. Any missing/lost/expired diagnostic coverage on either side invalidates the cell; known failures remain failures even if coverage is incomplete. |
| DELETE busy | Report all reader/writer public/native busy counts/rates; no busy-rate ceiling. A busy refusal is not successful retrieval and cannot meet success minimums or hide behind partial fallback. |
| Writer coexistence | Report capture/review public-write p50/p95/max, per-write close/full lifecycle, production busy/deadline settings and commit counts in every load cell. **No reader-induced public-write deadline overrun/timeout**, including canceled-native cleanup lag. No new numeric writer p95 or throughput gate; paired throughput ratio is report-only. |
| Functional/bytes | Every successful result matches the frozen selected-projection oracle, v1.1 request/service checks and ref/snapshot fences, exact counter/byte bounds. Unexpected errors remain failures regardless of their latency. |
| Language retrieval | Frozen zh/ja/mixed known-answer and deterministic ranking checks gate correctness. Report recall@5/@12 and known misses; supplied smoke questions are not a benchmark and 92% is not a new Japanese/mixed quality threshold. |
| Size/backup/verification | Report sidecar main/WAL/SHM/total/peak bytes at **both required world sizes with Cards**, plus all §8.4 backup/restore/build/deep-verify/fresh-reader-verify times. No time/size threshold was supplied for these outputs. |

### 10.4 Freshness and availability gates

Pre-register a continuous-writer workload and the explicit cron-renewed pump settings. After the **first completed certified build** of both required intents, count **all scheduled normal index-dependent `findCandidates` and `contextRead` attempts** in the fixed owner/readSet/type/intent cell. The denominator includes errors, timeouts, stale/unavailable results and fallback invocations; exact evidence-only calls are not allowed to dilute it. No exclusion of maintenance/dirty/cron-gap intervals after the initial build. Report all-attempt error categories separately. Require at least **100 index-dependent attempts per availability cell** across its registered windows; otherwise inconclusive.

**Not-ready fraction ≤1%** is `(index_unavailable + failures explicitly caused by stale/unvalidated/incomplete index state) / all scheduled normal index-dependent attempts`, with mutually exclusive per-attempt classification; timeouts/other errors are reported and remain subject to their independent gates. Fallback success is still a not-ready engine attempt. Overflow due solely to deliberate namespace selection is a separate scope-partial condition, not index-not-ready; report it and do not mix it into four-namespace launch coverage.

**New Memory or Card searchable within 30 s p95 under continuous writes:** record the canonical commit at which each synthetic item first becomes eligible for the tested intent (for current Cards, confirmed and eligible). Use pre-registered unique lexical probes whose expected top-24 membership is known from canonical fixture contents, and probe from fresh authorized handles at ≤1 s intervals, without repair/catch-up in a probe. Latency is from eligible canonical commit to the **first successful engine search containing the exact new ref/revision**, including cron wait, scheduling, build/catch-up and probe delay. Fallback is not searchable evidence. Keep continuous writers running until at least 30 s after the final eligible commit; register a bounded final observation/drain ceiling before the run. Items never observed by that ceiling are right-censored, retained as +infinity for the gate quantile, not deleted from the population. Report counts, finite/max and censored delays by type/intent/size/mode; require **≥100 eligible committed targets per applicable type/intent cell** or mark inconclusive. v18 has Memory targets only; mixed launch cells require both types. The nearest-rank p95 must be ≤30 s in every applicable cell.

Strict fresh-fence semantics may expose insufficient maintenance capacity under sustained writes. These gates are requirements, **not measured feasibility claims**; a failing implementation must improve background scheduling/index architecture within this contract or return for explicit contract review, not serve stale/partial-built lexical indexes or relax the targets. Partial namespace overflow remains distinct from index freshness.

A 2 s single-repetition smoke validates harness operation only. A failed, invalid, inconclusive or missing required production/platform cell prevents a blanket pass. Preserve raw attempt/telemetry records and actual process exits; report failure and recovery options without automatically restarting an expensive run.

## 11. Reviewable implementation packages and product integration

The non-author contract audit precedes **all** implementation. Keep defaults opt-in at every package, with an installed-artifact handoff and actual check exit codes. Each package has its own review and release capability statement; earlier packages do not imply later requirements are complete.

| Package | Scope and dependency | What the product can integrate afterward |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **P2a — Worker, request authority, deadlines and supervised opening** | Prerequisite: product signs off the six v1.1 adapter checks. Deliver request-bound credentials/two synchronous currentness samples, separate service-credential rejection, supervisor/process-wide cleanup ownership and bounded acknowledged history, independent native telemetry, async deadline/abort-aware canonical fetch and capabilities. Keep baseline one/two namespaces and DELETE; no production sidecar builder yet. Lexical-only; semantic owner decision remains pending. | **Capabilities and canonical exact evidence fetch only**, v18 Memories/v23+ Cards, personal plus at most one project, no index. Integrate fresh request identity, disconnect, failure/drain ownership and shutdown. Candidates/assembly remain unavailable; a test cache is not a production build route. Partial C-1 only; no authority-vector registry or final-read synchronization claim. |
| **P2b — Production index lifecycle and validation handoff** | Depends on P2a. Supplies the first production builder, separate product sidecar with immutable captured-generation certificates and post-commit readiness/completion checks, maintenance Worker/maxItems/maxMs API, closed status, cron-renewed supervised pump, current/history prebuild, no migration and backup/restore rebuild. No request maintenance; lexical first. | Real candidates/assembly now become integrable on P2a's one/two-namespace DELETE scope, using production-built indexes and fresh readers; keep v18 Memory-only. Integrate existing-path partial fallback without new UI copy. C-1 search/assembly and C-3 are available for that scope, subject to package gates. |
| **P2c — Owner-wide selection, projection and fusion** | Depends on P2a/P2b. Widen both validators/mapping/fences/certificates to 32 selected namespaces; add recent-projects-v1 overflow partial reporting, pooled-statistics/ranking oracle and projection prebuild for active selections. No new RRF preset. | One personal-plus-N lookup in DELETE, deterministic visible partial coverage for heavy owners, full four-namespace mixed-type gates on v23+. C-2 and the complete DELETE product path are available after qualification; production v18 remains Memory-only. |
| **P2d — WAL policy and complete qualification** | **Code dependencies: P2a/P2b** for supervisor/native telemetry/request checks and sidecar lifecycle. Narrow-scope WAL changes remain independently reviewable; opt-in qualification includes independently drained writer telemetry. **Full acceptance/release dependencies: P2a+P2b+P2c**, v1.1 adapter verification, production runtime/volume and all §10 gates, including owner-wide latency/freshness/availability. | WAL-capable reader/maintainer on qualified configurations. Full C-1–C-4 owner-wide claim requires all four packages and complete production evidence. Production stays DELETE until owner authorization after the baseline re-run; narrower WAL tests are not full acceptance. |

Product glue in the inaccessible private repository remains product-owned: `ask_cairn`/website adapters, authentication/bootstrap, fixed owner/project enumeration, non-model `_meta` retention, maintenance scheduling, not-ready UX and rollout switches. This plan specifies that boundary rather than claiming those changes are implemented here.

Full population/interval rules in §10; these numbers are no longer open. |
